{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✗}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2})=4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime},\hskip 22.99988pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2})=4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime}

(31)

The tokeniser with vocabulary
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}
will then compress each string in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
to
2
symbols, while
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✗}}}
will compress the
t
strings
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}
with unassigned variables to 3 symbols.
This will lead to a total compression of:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✗}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3})=(2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+t){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime},\hskip 22.99988pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3})=2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime}

(32)

Finally, the
t
doubly assigned tokens of the form
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}
(which
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}
does not contain) appear at most three times in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4}
and will lead to at most one symbol being saved, leading to a bound:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✗}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4})\leq 3t

(33)

Putting these compressed lengths together, we get:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✗}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}})\leq 3t-t{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime}

(34)

As
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime}>3
, this difference is smaller than zero, implying that
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}
improves on
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✗}}}
. This shows a contradiction, which completes our proof.
∎

LemmaProofStep 4
.

(Step
4
).

If an optimal tokeniser achieves a compressed length of at most

329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}
, the original
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
instance is satisfiable, i.e.:

\displaystyle\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}})\leq 329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}\Big)\implies{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})

(35)

Proof.

Given steps
1
to
3
, we know that an optimal tokeniser will be
sat
-compliant.
We will now denote this optimal tokeniser’s vocabulary by
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}
and use
appendix

B
to extract a
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
assignment
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathpzc{x}}^{\star}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}})
which corresponds to this tokeniser’s vocabulary.
From the previous proof steps we see that any
sat
-compliant tokeniser achieves the following compressed length in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3})=4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime}+2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime}=329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}

(36)

Now, note that a character-string
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4}
will be:
compressed to two symbols if at least one of the tokens
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}
exists, or
compressed to three symbols if neither exists.
Equivalently, a clause
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}\lor{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}
in
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
is:
satisfied if either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}
evaluates to true, or
not satisfied if both evaluate to false.
Given our construction of function
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}
above, one of
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
’s clauses will be satisfied if and only if its corresponding string in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4}
is compressed to two symbols.
We can thus state that:

\displaystyle\bigg({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4})=3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{\star}}\bigg)\iff\left(\sum_{i=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbbm{1}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathpzc{x}}^{\star}}}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}\lor{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}\}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{\star}}\right)

(37)

Given the construction of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
as
329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}
, we conclude that a
sat
-compliant tokeniser which compresses the full dataset to at least that size can be mapped to a
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
assignment which satisfies at least
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}
clauses.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of
Theorem

2

See
2

Proof.

For this proof, we rely on a result by
2
;
3

that, for specific instances of
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}=2016n
clauses, it is
\mathsf{NP}
-hard to distinguish whether at least
(2012-\varepsilon)n
or at most
(2011+\varepsilon)n
of these clauses are satisfiable, for any
\varepsilon>0
.
We will denote this
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
gap problem by
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{-},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{+}))
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{-}=(2011+\varepsilon)n
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{+}=(2012-\varepsilon)n
.
We can now prove the
\mathsf{NP}
-hardness of the binary direct tokenisation gap problem by reducing
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
’s gap problem to it.
To this end, we rely on a reduction identical to
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R1}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
, but where we define:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{-}

\displaystyle=329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{-}\hskip 22.99988pt

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{+}

\displaystyle=329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{+}

(38)

\displaystyle=329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2011+\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}

\displaystyle=329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2012-\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}

lemmas

1
and
2
trivially show the validity of this reduction:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{-},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{+}))\iff{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\text{\pointer}}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{-},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{+}))

(39)

which holds since
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{+})\iff{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\text{\pointer}}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{+})
and the same for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{-}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{-}
.
It is therefore
\mathsf{NP}
-hard to distinguish whether a dataset can be compressed to at most
329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2012-\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}
symbols, or if at least
329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2011+\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}
symbols remain (with an allowed vocabulary size
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}=5{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}
).
Since each variable occurs exactly three times in
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
, we have that
\frac{3}{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}
.
We now compute a lower bound on the best achievable compression ratio:

\displaystyle\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{-}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{+}}

\displaystyle=\frac{329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2011+\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}}{329{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2012-\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}}

(40a)

\displaystyle=\frac{667-\frac{6033+3\varepsilon}{2016}}{667-\frac{6036-\varepsilon}{2016}}

(40b)

\displaystyle=\frac{1338639-3\varepsilon}{1338636+3\varepsilon}

(40c)

\displaystyle=\frac{446213-\varepsilon}{446212+\varepsilon}

(40d)

Thus, binary direct tokenisation cannot be approximated in polynomial time with an approximation ratio better than
\frac{446213}{446212}>1.000002
, unless
\mathsf{P}=\mathsf{NP}
.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Forward Step of
Theorem

3

We first need another lemma in preparation for the actual proof of this forward step.
Note that
2
produces character-strings
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}
, with form
\{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}^{j}\mid 1\leq j\leq 2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}\}
, which our tokeniser must compress.
However, a merge-sequence
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}=\bigcirc_{j=1}^{2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}}]
does not compress all these targets into a single symbol; character-string
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}
, for instance, would be merged into
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}\rangle}
by the first merge
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}}
in this sequence, and merge
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{3}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}}
would not be applied to it.
Thus, we need to describe a more unwieldy merge sequence to achieve this with the same number of merges.
We do so in
section

D.1
, where we show that exactly
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1
merges are required to compress all these strings into a single symbol.
With this, we now prove the forward step of
Theorem

3

in the following lemma.

Lemma 3
.

If a
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
instance is satisfiable, then the
B-2-TOK
instance output by
2
is also satisfiable. Formally:

{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})\implies{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\uparrow}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R1}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}))
.

Proof.

Assume this
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
instance of the
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
decision problem is satisfiable, i.e., that
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
is true. We must prove that in this case,
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\uparrow}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R1}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}))
is also true.
We define the following list of merges which, as shown in
1
, compresses every target of type
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}
into a single token:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{1}=({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})\circ\underbrace{\bigcirc_{j=1}^{\lfloor\log_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}\rfloor}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}})]\circ\bigcirc_{j=1}^{\lfloor\log_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}\rfloor}\bigcirc_{j^{\prime}=1}^{2^{j}-1}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j^{\prime}}}})]}_{\text{$2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1$ merges which compress each ${\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}$ and ${\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}$ to a single token}}

(41)

Note that the merge
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}
is independent of the merges on
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}
and could thus be placed at any point in the sequence.
We also define the following lists of merges, which will be included in any satisfying solution to the tokenisation problem:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{2}=\bigcirc_{j=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}})

\displaystyle,({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})]\hskip 22.99988pt{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{4}=\bigcirc_{j=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}),({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}})]

(42a)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{6}=\bigcirc_{j=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}}),({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})]

(42b)

Now, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathpzc{x}}^{\star}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}^{\star}_{j}}\}_{j=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}
be any satisfying solution to the
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
instance
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
. We define the following instance-specific merges:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{3}=\BigCirc_{j=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}\left[\begin{array}[]{lr}({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})&\mathtt{if}\,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}^{\star}_{j}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{T}}\\
({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})&\mathtt{else}\end{array}\right],\hskip 22.99988pt{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{5}=\BigCirc_{j=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}\left[\begin{array}[]{lr}({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})&\mathtt{if}\,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}^{\star}_{j}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{T}}\\
({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})&\mathtt{else}\end{array}\right]

In words, we include merges
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}^{\star}_{j}}
is true, or

{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}^{\star}_{j}}
is false.
We then create a merge sequence by concatenating these lists in order:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}={\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{1}\circ{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{2}\circ{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{3}\circ{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{4}\circ{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{5}\circ{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{6}

(47)

This gives us a total of
|{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}|={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}=10{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}
merges.
Now we just need to count the symbols output by this solution to check whether the bound is satisfied.

By applying the merges
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}
, each string in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}
will be compressed into a single symbol, obtaining:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\uparrow}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1})=(1+8{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}

(48)

For each pair of strings
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
, one is compressed into a single symbol while the other is only compressed to two symbols—the one with
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}}
is compressed into a single symbol
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{j}^{\star}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{T}}
and the one with
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}}
otherwise.
The same is true for each pair of strings
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}}
, also in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
.
We thus have that, for each variable
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}_{j}
, the strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
will occupy a total of
(1+2+1+2){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime}
symbols, and:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\uparrow}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2})=6{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}

(49)

Similarly, each string in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4}
will be compressed into only 2 symbols after this tokeniser is applied to it.
We thus have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\uparrow}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3})=4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J},\hskip 22.99988pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\uparrow}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4})=4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}

(50)

Finally, we have the strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{5}
.
These strings are constructed such that they will be compressed into 2 symbols if either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}
evaluates to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{T}}
, and kept with 3 symbols otherwise; see
table

1
for a detailed
simulation of why this is the case.
We thus have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\uparrow}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{5})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}}\left(3-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbbm{1}}\left\{\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}_{j}\phantom{\neg}\,\,\mathtt{and}\,\,({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}),({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}\\
\mathrm{or}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}=\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}_{j}\,\,\mathtt{and}\,\,({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}),({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}\\
\mathrm{or}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}_{j^{\prime}}\phantom{\neg}\,\,\mathtt{and}\,\,({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}),({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j^{\prime}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}\\
\mathrm{or}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}=\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}_{j^{\prime}}\,\,\mathtt{and}\,\,({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j^{\prime}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}),({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}\end{array}\!\!\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\sum_{i=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbbm{1}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathpzc{x}}^{\star}}}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}\lor{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}\}

(51h)

\displaystyle\leq 3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}

(51i)

where, by construction, we have a merge in our sequence (e.g.,
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}
) if and only if its value is in a satisfying assignment (e.g.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}^{\star}_{j}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{T}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}^{\star}_{j}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{F}}
, respectively).
Summing together the lengths in
eqs.

48
,
49
,
50
and
51
, we get that:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\uparrow}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}})\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}=(1+8{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}+(6{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime}+4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime}+4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime\prime})\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+3\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}

(52)

which concludes the proof.
∎

Assignment

Condition

{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}}

{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\uparrow}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{1}]({\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}})

Merges Optimally Compress the
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}

{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}^{j}
Strings

SubLemma 1
.

Given character-strings
\{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}^{j}\mid 1\leq j\leq 2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}\}
, an optimal bottom-up tokeniser
requires exactly
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1
merges to encode all these strings into a single token.
9
9

9

Note the same is true for both optimal direct tokenisers, and optimal OPE tokenisers (defined in
section

5.2
).

Proof.

We establish the result in two steps:

1

we prove that at
least

2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1
merges are
required
to reduce these strings to a single token;

2

we prove that
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1
merges are
sufficient
to reduce these character-strings to a single token.

Given these upper and lower bounds, we conclude exactly
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1
merges are required to reduce these character-strings to a single token. This completes the proof.
∎

SubLemmaProofStep 1
.

(Step
1
).

Given character-strings
\{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}^{j}\mid 1\leq j\leq 2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}\}
, an optimal bottom-up tokeniser requires
at
least

2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1
merges to encode all these strings into a single token.

Proof.

The target set contains
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}
distinct values, one of which is the base symbol
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}
. Whenever a target becomes a single token through a merge, that merge must combine exactly two tokens whose concatenation equals that specific target. Since all targets are distinct, this concatenation cannot simultaneously equal any other target. Hence, a single merge can complete at most one target.
It follows that at least
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1
merges are required to reduce all targets to single tokens.
∎

SubLemmaProofStep 2
.

(Step
2
).

Given character-strings
\{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}^{j}\mid 1\leq j\leq 2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}\}
, there exists an explicit merge sequence that reduces all these strings to single tokens in exactly
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1
merges.

Proof.

For the matching upper bound, we will construct an explicit merge sequence,
composed of two types of merges:

1.

Binary stage.

For each power of two up to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}
—i.e., with
j\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}
such that
2^{j}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}
—incrementally include binary merges as
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}}
.

2.

Extension stage.

For each power of two up to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}
—i.e., with
j\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}
such that
2^{j}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}
—incrementally create non-binary merges by merging binary tokens
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}

with non-binary ones
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j^{\prime}}}
(with
j^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}
such that
j^{\prime}<2^{j}
).
These merges will thus be
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j^{\prime}}}}
.
10
10

10

As we will see in the proof, the smallest non-fully merged value always consists of only two symbols, such that the “rightmost” tiebreaker is not necessary.

These merges are combined as:

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigcirc_{j=1}^{\lfloor\log_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}\rfloor}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}})]}_{\text{binary merges}}\hskip 22.99988pt\circ\hskip 22.99988pt\underbrace{\bigcirc_{j=1}^{\lfloor\log_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}\rfloor}\bigcirc_{j^{\prime}=1}^{2^{j}-1}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j^{\prime}}}})]}_{\text{extension merges}}

(53)

Note that, as merges are created incrementally, token
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}
always exists when merge
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}}
is applied.
Similarly, for
j^{\prime}<2^{j}
, both token
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j^{\prime}}}
will exist when merge
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j^{\prime}}}}
is applied.
Finally, for
j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}<2^{j}
, tokens
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j^{\prime}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}}
will both be created before any extension merge with left-side
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}
is applied; thus, a merge
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j^{\prime}}}}
will never affect subword-string
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}}\rangle}
or
vice-versa
.
After these merges are applied, it is easy to see that each character-string
\{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}^{j}\mid 1\leq j\leq 2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}\}
will be represented as a single symbol.
As the merge-sequence above contains
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1
merges, this completes the proof.∎

Appendix E
Proof of Backward Step of
Theorem

3

We again start with defining some useful notions and redefine compliant tokenisers.

First, even though this section is about bottom-up tokenisers, we define a term addressing direct tokenisers, meaning this definition describes tokenisers with tokens instead of merges.
We will use this definition in our lemma to prove a fact about direct tokenisers, which we later generalise by showing that it applies to bottom-up tokenisers as well.
We define a
sat
-compliant (direct) tokeniser

to be any tokeniser which:
(i) contains all tokens of the form
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}
;
and
(ii) contains either
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}
for each
j\in\{1,\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}\}
.
Otherwise, we call the tokeniser
sat
-noncompliant
.

We further adapt the definition of 101-strings to also include all character-strings of the form
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}^{+}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}^{+}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}1}
.
Considering the datasets output by
2
, we know that
there are no 101-strings in dataset
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}
.
Further, we know that each unique 101-string appears in datasets
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4}
exactly
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime}
,
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime}
, and
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime\prime}
times, respectively, and exactly 3 times in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{5}
(this is due to us working with the three-occurrences variant of
MAX2SAT
and to the fact that
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}={\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}^{2j-1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}={\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}0}^{2j}
).
We now prove the following lemma.

Lemma 4
.

If the
B-2-TOK
instance output by
2
is satisfiable, the
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
instance which generated it is as well. Formally:

{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\uparrow}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}))\implies{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
.

Proof.

Assume this
B-2-TOK
instance
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})
—where
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})={\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
—is satisfiable, i.e., that
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\uparrow}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}))
evaluates to true.
We must prove that, in this case,
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
also evaluates to true.
Now, let
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}
be an arbitrary
optimal solution to
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})
.
We know, by definition, that:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\uparrow}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}))\iff\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\uparrow}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}})\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}\Big)

(54)

We can thus prove this lemma by showing the following implication:

\displaystyle\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\uparrow}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}})\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}\Big)\implies{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})

(55)

When comparing two bottom-up tokenisers, things quickly get messy, because we have to not only consider the merges, but also their order.
For this reason, we show that
sat
-compliant direct tokenisers can be transformed into bottom-up tokenisers without loss of compression quality.
Thus, for the
sat
-compliant tokeniser, we can consider the direct tokeniser instead.
The key idea for this to work is that all target strings are hit via a sequence of merges such that each intermediate merge also hits a target (which has high multiplicity), such that this target must also be included as a token by a direct tokeniser.
We also compare to
sat
-noncompliant direct tokenisers, which are by definition at least as strong as bottom-up tokenisers; thus we can compute upper bounds on their performance.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}
again be the optimal direct tokeniser for
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})
.
We will proceed in six steps:

1

we prove that
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}
must include all tokens of the form

{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}

we prove that

;

3

we prove that

may only include, for each

, either token
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}
, and either token
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}

4

{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}

{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}

5

we prove that for any

{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}
, there exists a merge sequence
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}
with the same performance;

6

finally, we prove that if
\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\uparrow}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}})\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}\Big)
, we can build a variable assignment which satisfies this
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
instance
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
.

Note that, together, steps
1
to
4
show that

without losing compression, showing an equivalence between the two types of tokenisers for these reduced instances; and in step

for the instance

∎

.

(62)

For strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
: if the entire string is in the vocabulary, it is encoded as a single token; else, it is represented with two symbols.
Under
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}
, there are

tokens covering strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}

(63)

For strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{5}
, an argument similar to the previous step applies:
(i) only tokens containing 101-strings can compress these datasets;
(ii) each 101-string appears, as a prefix or suffix, at most
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime}+2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime\prime}+3
times in them;
(iii) each 101-string will lead to at most two symbols being saved.
As
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✗}}}
differs from
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}
in

tokens, we get that it will improve on it by at most:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{5})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✗}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{5})\leq 2t(2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime}+2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime\prime}+3)

(64)

Summing together the compression on all datasets, we get that their difference is bounded by:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✗}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}})\leq 2t(2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime}+2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime\prime}+3)-t{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime}

(65)

As

, this difference is smaller than zero, implying that

∎

, this difference is smaller than zero, implying that
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✓}}}
improves on
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\text{✗}}}
. This shows a contradiction, which completes our proof.
∎

LemmaProofStep 5
.

(Step
5
).

A
sat
-compliant direct tokeniser can be transformed into a bottom-up tokeniser without changing its performance.
As any optimal direct tokeniser is
sat
-compliant, this implies:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\uparrow}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}))\iff{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\text{\pointer}}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}))

(75)

Proof.

As every bottom-up tokeniser can be interpreted as a direct tokeniser with the same vocabulary size and a possibly suboptimal application of its tokens, it holds that:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\uparrow}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}))\implies{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\text{\pointer}}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}))

(76)

This is to say, direct tokenisers always compress at least as well as bottom-up tokenisers, when allowed the same vocabulary size.

Given a
sat
-compliant direct tokeniser, we first describe how to transform it into a bottom-up tokeniser.
We always include merges:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{1}

\displaystyle=({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})\circ\bigcirc_{j=1}^{\lfloor\log(2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1)\rfloor}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{i}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{i}}}})]\circ\bigcirc_{j=1}^{\lfloor\log(2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1)\rfloor}\bigcirc_{j^{\prime}=1}^{2^{j}-1}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{2^{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}0}^{j^{\prime}}}})]

(77a)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigcirc_{j=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}}),({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})]

(77b)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{4}

\displaystyle=\bigcirc_{j=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}),({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}})]

(77c)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{6}

\displaystyle=\bigcirc_{j=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}[({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}}),({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}})]

and additionally, depending on which tokens are included in the direct tokeniser’s vocabulary

.
For

,
table

1
shows that each string which could be reduced to two symbols by the direct tokeniser is also reduced to two symbols by the bottom-up tokeniser.
Thus, we get:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\uparrow}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}))\iff{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\text{\pointer}}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}))
