which completes this proof.
∎

LemmaProofStep 6
.

(Step
6
).

If an optimal (direct) tokeniser achieves a compressed length of at most
5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}
, the original
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
instance is satisfiable, i.e.:

\displaystyle\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}})\leq 5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}\Big)\implies{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})

(80)

Proof.

Given steps
1
to
4
, we know that an optimal tokeniser will be
sat
-compliant.
We will now denote this optimal tokeniser’s vocabulary by
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}
and use
appendix

B
to extract a
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
assignment
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathpzc{x}}^{\star}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}})
which corresponds to this tokeniser’s vocabulary.
From the previous proof steps, we know that any
sat
-compliant tokeniser achieves the following compressed length in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4}
:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4})

\displaystyle=(8{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+1){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}+6{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime}+4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime}+4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}^{\prime\prime\prime}

(81a)

\displaystyle=(8\cdot 575+6\cdot 115+4\cdot 23+4\cdot 4){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575

(81b)

\displaystyle=5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575

(81c)

Now, note that any target string in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{5}
will be: (i)
compressed to two symbols either if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}_{j}
and tokens
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{T}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}

exist or if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}
is
\neg{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}_{j}
and tokens
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{x}^{\mathtt{F}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}1}
exist; or (ii)
compressed to three symbols if neither case is satisfied.
Equivalently, a clause
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}\lor{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}
in
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
is:
satisfied if either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}
evaluates to true; or
not satisfied if both evaluate to false.
Given our construction of function
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}
above, one of
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
’s clauses will be satisfied if and only if its corresponding string in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{4}
is compressed to two symbols.
We can thus state that:

\displaystyle\bigg({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}],{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{5})=3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{\star}}\bigg)\iff\left(\sum_{i=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbbm{1}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathpzc{x}}^{\star}}}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{1}\lor{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}_{i}^{2}\}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma^{\star}}\right)

(82)

Given the construction of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
as
5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}
, we conclude that a
sat
-compliant tokeniser which compresses the full dataset to at most that size can be mapped to a
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
assignment which satisfies at least
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}
clauses.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix F
Proof of
theorem

4

See
4

Proof.

For this proof, we again rely on the result by
2
;
3

that, for specific instances of
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}=2016n
clauses, it is
\mathsf{NP}
-hard to distinguish whether at least
(2012-\varepsilon)n
or at most
(2011+\varepsilon)n
of these clauses are satisfiable, for any
\varepsilon>0
.
We will denote this
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
gap problem by
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{-},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{+}))
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{-}=(2011+\varepsilon)n
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{+}=(2012-\varepsilon)n
.
We can now prove the
\mathsf{NP}
-hardness of the binary bottom-up tokenisation gap problem by reducing
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
’s gap problem to it.
To this end, we rely on a reduction identical to
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
, but where we define:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{-}

\displaystyle=5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{-}\hskip 22.99988pt

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{+}

\displaystyle=5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{+}

(83)

\displaystyle=5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2011+\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}

\displaystyle=5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2012-\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}

lemmas

3
and
4
trivially show the validity of this reduction:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{-},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{+}))\iff{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\uparrow}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{-},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{+}))

(84)

which holds since
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{3OM2S}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{+})\iff{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\uparrow}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{+})
and the same for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma}^{-}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{-}
.
It is therefore
\mathsf{NP}
-hard to distinguish whether a dataset can be compressed to at most
5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2012-\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}
symbols, or if at least
5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2011+\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}
symbols remain (with an allowed vocabulary size
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}=10{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}
).
Since each variable occurs exactly three times in any
3-OCC-MAX2SAT
instance, we have that
\frac{3}{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}
.
We now compute a lower bound on the best achievable compression ratio:

\displaystyle\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{-}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}^{+}}

\displaystyle=\frac{5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2011+\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}}{5398{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575+3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}-\frac{2012-\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}}

(85a)

\displaystyle=\frac{10805{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575-\frac{6033+3\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}{10805{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+575-\frac{6036-3\varepsilon}{2016}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}

(85b)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{10805-\frac{6033+3\varepsilon^{\prime}}{2016}}{10805-\frac{6036-3\varepsilon^{\prime}}{2016}}

additive 575 omitted in
\varepsilon^{\prime}
for sufficiently large
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}

(85c)

\displaystyle=\frac{7258949-\varepsilon^{\prime}}{7258948+\varepsilon^{\prime}}

(85d)

We conclude that binary bottom-up tokenisation cannot be approximated in polynomial time with an approximation ratio better than
\frac{7258949}{7258948}>1.0000001
, unless
\mathsf{P}=\mathsf{NP}
.
∎

Appendix G
Proof that Unary Direct Tokenisation is in NP

A decision problem is in the nondeterministic polynomial-time class (
\mathsf{NP}
) if it can be verified in polynomial time in the presence of a
certificate
: a string designed to verify that the current instance is a “yes”-instance, typically encoding an optimal solution to its search problem.
When inputs are represented as strings, the following lemma follows trivially from the unbounded-alphabet case discussed by
19
.
Their proof, however, relies on the explicit computation of the direct tokenisation function:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}]({\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}})=

\displaystyle\argmin_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}s}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}^{*}}|{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}s}}}|,\hskip 22.99988pt\mathrm{s.t.}\,\,{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\circ}}}{{=}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}s}}}

(86)

While we can efficiently compute this when inputs are given in string form, this is not known to be the case for the string-length representation.
In this case, the optimal application of tokens corresponds to the change-making problem, which is itself weakly
\mathsf{NP}
-hard, as shown by
13
.
Thus, we now include the optimal application of tokens
as a part of the certificate, in order to prove that this decision problem is still in
\mathsf{NP}
when inputs are represented as string-lengths.

Lemma 5
.

The unary direct tokenisation decision problem is in
\mathsf{NP}
.

Proof.

We use as certificate a set of string-lengths composing the tokeniser’s vocabulary
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
, as well as a set
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{Z}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{z}}_{m}\}_{m=1}^{M}
, where each
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{z}}_{m}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}^{|{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}|}

shows how many tokens of each length should be used to tokenise each string in the dataset
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
.
Verifying this certificate then simply requires computing the sum of tokens used for each target.

If
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}|\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}
, each
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
can be included as a token, and thus all entries in our dataset can be compressed into single token; consequently, the certificate can simply be empty and we verify the problem’s satisfiability by checking whether
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}\geq|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}|
holds.
Assuming
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}|>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}
—and therefore that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}
’s value is polynomial in the input—we have that the certificate
also has polynomial length, and that, in particular, the size of
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{Z}}
is bounded by
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}\,\log{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}_{\mathrm{max}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}_{\mathrm{max}}
is the maximum string-length in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
.
Thus, all that is left is to compute the sum of
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{Z}}
and check whether

\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{z}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{Z}}}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{sum}}({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{z}})\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
.∎

Appendix H
Proof of Forward Step of
Theorem

5

Lemma 6
.

If a
vertex-cover
instance is satisfiable, then the
D-1-TOK
instance output by
3

is also satisfiable. Formally:

{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{VC}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi})\implies{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\text{\pointer}}^{1}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R3}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}))
.

Proof.

Suppose the given instance of
vertex-cover
is satisfiable.
Then there exists a vertex cover
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}}^{\star}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}}
which uses
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}
vertices.
As a consequence, we can choose as tokens those with the following lengths:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{j}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}}\}\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}B}\}\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}}^{\star}}\}

(87)

We have that every vertex-string in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}
is covered by a single token, either of length
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}_{j}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}B}
.

All
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}
cover-strings
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}a}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j}}}

in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
which encode a vertex that belongs to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}}^{\star}}
are also covered by a single token.
The remaining
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}
cover-strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
are covered by 2 tokens, as for every target of length
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j}}={\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{enc}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j}\big)+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}^{3}
there exist the tokens of length
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{j}}={\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{enc}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j}\big)
and
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}B}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}^{3}
.

As
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}}^{\star}}
is a vertex cover, we have that for every edge
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j^{\prime}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}}
at least one of the vertices belongs to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}}^{\star}}
.
It follows that for every edge-string of length

{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j^{\prime}}}={\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{enc}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j}\big)+{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{enc}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j^{\prime}}\big)+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}^{3}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
,
at least one of the tokens of length

{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j}}={\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{enc}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j}\big)+{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}
or

{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j^{\prime}}}={\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{enc}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j^{\prime}}\big)+{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}

belongs to
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
.
Thus, all edge-strings are covered by two tokens.

We can now count the number of symbols used in each dataset:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}
uses
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+1
symbols;

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
uses
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}
symbols; and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
uses
2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}
symbols.
This gives us a total of
3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}+1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
symbols, which satisfies this tokenisation instance.
Thus, we have that
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\text{\pointer}}^{1}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{T}}
.
∎

Appendix I
Proof of Backward Step of
Theorem

5

Lemma 7
.

If the
D-1-TOK
instance output by
3
is satisfiable, then the
vertex-cover
instance which generated it is as well. Formally:

{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\text{\pointer}}^{1}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R3}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}))\implies{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{VC}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi})
.

Proof.

Assume this instance
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})
of
D-1-TOK
—where
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})={\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R3}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi})
—is satisfiable, i.e., that
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\text{\pointer}}^{1}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R3}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}))
evaluates to true.
We must prove that, in this case,
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{VC}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi})
also evaluates to true.
Now, let
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
be an arbitrary optimal solution to
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})
.
By construction, we have that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}=3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}+1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}
.
We proceed in four steps:

1

We prove that all target strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
are unique;

2

We prove that an optimal tokeniser must only include full target strings in its vocabulary;

3

We prove that an optimal tokeniser will include all target strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}
in its vocabulary;

4

We prove that, if an optimal tokeniser achieves compression
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
, then the instance of
vertex-cover
which was reduced to it is satisfiable.

These steps first show that an optimal tokeniser must admit a certain form (Steps
1
–
3
), and that from this form (Step
4
), we can deduce a valid vertex cover, which concludes the proof.
∎

LemmaProofStep 1
.

(Step
1
).

All strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
are unique.

Proof.

Now we show
that all strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
are unique.
These strings all have lengths:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{enc}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j_{1}}\big),\hskip 11.49994pt{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}^{4},\hskip 11.49994pt{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{enc}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j_{1}}\big)+{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B},\hskip 11.49994pt{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{2}}}={\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{enc}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j_{1}}\big)+{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{enc}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j_{2}}\big)+{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}

(88)

for
1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}
and with
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{enc}}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j_{1}}\big)=j_{1}+j_{1}^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}+j_{1}^{3}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}^{2}
.
Notably, our reduction defines
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}\gg{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}^{3}
and it will be useful to think about these lengths in base
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}
.
Let a number
(a,b,c,d,e)_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}
denote
a{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}^{4}+b{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}^{3}+c{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}^{2}+d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}^{1}+e
.
For example, we can write:

\displaystyle(a,b,c,d,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}-1)_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}+(a,0,0,0,1)_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}=(2a,b,c,d+1,0)_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}

(89)

We can similarly write in this base:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{1}}}=(0,0,j_{1}^{3},j_{1}^{2},j_{1})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}},\hskip 11.49994pt{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}=(1,0,0,0,0)_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}},\hskip 11.49994pt{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}}}=(1,0,j_{1}^{3},j_{1}^{2},j_{1})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}},

(90a)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{2}}}=(1,0,j_{1}^{3}\!+\!j_{2}^{3},j_{1}^{2}\!+\!j_{2}^{2},j_{1}\!+\!j_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}},\,\,\,\,{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}}}+{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{2}}}=(2,0,j_{1}^{3}\!+\!j_{2}^{3},j_{1}^{2}\!+\!j_{2}^{2},j_{1}\!+\!j_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}

(90b)

Two numbers are the same only if each “digit” in this base system is the same.
Given this structure, we see that
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}
are all unique string-lengths.
Further, the string-lengths
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}}}
are all different from one another.
It is left to show that:
(i) all string-lengths
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}}}
are different from all
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{2}}}
; and
(ii) that string-lengths
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{2}}}
are different among themselves.
Requirement (i) is proven by
2
, which shows that there is no set of numbers
j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}
for which
j_{1}=j_{2}+j_{3}
and
j_{1}^{2}=j_{2}^{2}+j_{3}^{2}
.
Requirement (ii) is proven by
3
, which shows that there is no set of numbers
j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}
for which
j_{1}+j_{2}=j_{3}+j_{4}
and
j_{1}^{2}+j_{2}^{2}=j_{3}^{2}+j_{4}^{2}
.
It follows that all strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
are unique.
∎

LemmaProofStep 2
.

(Step
2
).

An optimal tokeniser must only include full character-strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
and compress all other strings to two symbols.

Proof.

Note that, since all strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
are unique, the best compression one could possibly achieve would result from compressing

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}
strings into a single symbol, and the remaining

|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}|-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}
to two symbols.
As
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}|=2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}+1
, this (hypothetical) optimal compression would lead to:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathfrak{G}}_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{\mathtt{opt}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}})

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}+2(|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}|-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K})

(91a)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}+2(2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}+1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}-1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi})

(91b)

\displaystyle=3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I}+1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}

(91c)

As by assumption
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\text{\pointer}}^{1}}({\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{R3}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}))
evaluates to true, our tokeniser must achieve this compression, and is thus composed of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}
full strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
.
Further, it must compress all other strings to at most two symbols.
∎

LemmaProofStep 3
.

(Step
3
).

An optimal tokeniser selects every vertex-string in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}
as a token.

Proof.

Suppose that some vertex-string of length
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{1}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}

is not chosen as a token.
Then
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{1}}}
must be the sum of two tokens.
No tokens of cover- or vertex-strings (in datasets
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
) can be used, since such tokens contain a summand
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}
, which is significantly larger than
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{1}}}
.
Hence, both summands would have to also be vertex-strings
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{3}}}
. These string-lengths have values:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{1}}}=(1,0,j_{1}^{3},j_{1}^{2},j_{1})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}},\hskip 11.49994pt{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{2}}}=(1,0,j_{2}^{3},j_{2}^{2},j_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}},\hskip 11.49994pt{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{3}}}=(1,0,j_{3}^{3},j_{3}^{2},j_{3})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}

(92)

Again, by
2
, it is impossible that
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{2}}}+{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{3}}}
.
Thus, no target string in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}
can be covered by two other tokens; but, as argued in Step
2
, the tokeniser may use at most two symbols per target.
This concludes the proof that all character-strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}
must be included in the vocabulary
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
.
Further, every cover and edge-string is larger than
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}
, while all vertex-strings are significantly smaller than it;

{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}
thus cannot be written as two other tokens and must hence also be part of
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}
.
∎

LemmaProofStep 4
.

(Step
4
).

If an optimal tokeniser achieves compression
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
, then the original
vertex-cover
instance is satisfiable.

Proof.

Step
3
shows that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}+1
tokens in any optimal tokeniser must correspond to the target strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{1}
.
Using only these tokens, every target in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
needs two symbols, and every target in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
needs three symbols.
With step
2
, the remaining
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}
tokens must correspond to target strings from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
. We are going to show that: a token corresponding to an edge target can only contribute to itself; a token corresponding to a cover target can only contribute to itself and edge targets which include the vertex the cover consists of.
Without loss of generality, let
t
with
0\leq t\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}
of these remaining tokens be edge-strings (from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
) and let the remaining
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}-t
be cover-strings (from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
).

We are now going to show that a token from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
can only improve the solution by reducing its target string to a single token.
Pick any of the selected edge tokens, having a length of
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{2}}}=(1,0,j_{1}^{3}\!+\!j_{2}^{3},j_{1}^{2}\!+\!j_{2}^{2},j_{1}\!+\!j_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}
.
For this edge token to contribute to compressing a target string, it must be combined with another token; let its length be
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{\oplus}}
, such that their sum equals the length of one of the target strings already present in the dataset.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{2}}}
already contains a summand
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}
, the token
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{\oplus}}
cannot contain
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}
, as any target string length in the dataset is strictly less than
2{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}
.
As established in step
2
, tokens must correspond to target string-lengths themselves.
Since any non-vertex target string contains a summand
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}B}
, the additional token
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{\oplus}}
must therefore be a vertex token, with length
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{\oplus}}=(0,0,j_{\oplus}^{3},j_{\oplus}^{2},j_{\oplus})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}
.
Now, assume that the edge token
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{2}}}
is combined with this vertex token
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{\oplus}}
to compress an arbitrary target from one of the three datasets:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{j_{3}}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{2}}}+{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{\oplus}}\iff

(93a)

\displaystyle(0,0,j_{3}^{3},j_{3}^{2},j_{3})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}=(1,0,j_{\oplus}^{3}+j_{1}^{3}+j_{2}^{3},j_{\oplus}^{2}+j_{1}^{2}+j_{2}^{2},j_{\oplus}+j_{1}+j_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{3}}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{2}}}+{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{\oplus}}\iff

(93b)

\displaystyle(1,0,j_{3}^{3},j_{3}^{2},j_{3})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}=(1,0,j_{\oplus}^{3}+j_{1}^{3}+j_{2}^{3},j_{\oplus}^{2}+j_{1}^{2}+j_{2}^{2},j_{\oplus}+j_{1}+j_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{3}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{4}}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}j_{2}}}+{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{\oplus}}\iff

(93c)

\displaystyle(1,0,j_{3}^{3}+j_{4}^{3},j_{3}^{2}+j_{4}^{2},j_{3}+j_{4})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}=(1,0,j_{\oplus}^{3}+j_{1}^{3}+j_{2}^{3},j_{\oplus}^{2}+j_{1}^{2}+j_{2}^{2},j_{\oplus}+j_{1}+j_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}

The first case clearly cannot be satisfied, as any vertex target has length strictly smaller than any edge target.
Additionally, the two other cases cannot be satisfied per
5
and
4
.
In other words, this shows that edge-strings cannot contribute to any other target value.

We are now left with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}-t
tokens formed of cover-strings.
Recall that using only the tokens obtained from step
3
, every target in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
needs two symbols, and every target in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
needs three symbols.
Having the newly obtained tokens corresponding to a target from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
we will show that they can only be applied optimally on: themselves, resulting in one symbol used; target strings from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
, reducing the symbols to two.
Any other application of these tokens would not yield a better compression, as improving compression
is only possible if the token obtained from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}
compresses a target from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{2}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}_{3}
other than itself to a single token.
But step
1
shows that all target strings are unique, meaning a token cannot reduce any target string apart from itself to a single symbol.

Finally, these selected tokens must be used, in conjunction with vertex-strings, to compress all the remaining edge-strings to two tokens.
Note that only composing a cover and a vertex-string can compress an edge-string to two symbols:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{1}}}+{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{2}}}=(0,0,j_{1}^{3}\!+\!j_{2}^{3},j_{1}^{2}\!+\!j_{2}^{2},j_{1}\!+\!j_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}

(94a)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}}}+{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{2}}}=(2,0,j_{1}^{3}\!+\!j_{2}^{3},j_{1}^{2}\!+\!j_{2}^{2},j_{1}\!+\!j_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}

(94b)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}}}+{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}{{}_{j_{2}}}=(1,0,j_{1}^{3}\!+\!j_{2}^{3},j_{1}^{2}\!+\!j_{2}^{2},j_{1}\!+\!j_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}

(94c)

Additionally, an edge-string can only be compressed by a cover- or vertex-string subword which contains exactly the vertices that the edge consists of.
Assume there exists another set of vertex- and cover-strings such that their tokens
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{j_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{j_{2}}}
can compress an edge-string consisting of different vertices
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{3}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{4}}}
. Then we have that:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{3}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{4}}}={\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{j_{1}}}+{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}{{}_{j_{2}}}\iff(1,0,j_{3}^{3}+j_{4}^{3},j_{3}^{2}+j_{4}^{2},j_{3}+j_{4})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}

\displaystyle=(1,0,j_{1}^{3}+j_{2}^{3},j_{1}^{2}+j_{2}^{2},j_{1}+j_{2})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}

(95)

From
3
it follows that this cannot be the case.
This means that, for each edge-string of length
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{2}}}
not in our tokeniser, we must have a subword (of length either
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{2}}}
) which “covers” it to obtain our target compression.
Consider thus, the subgraph
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
, where:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}}\setminus\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j_{2}})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{2}}}\notin{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}\}

(96)

There exists a vertex cover of size
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}-t
for this subgraph composed of vertices
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}\}
.
Now, if we expand this set of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}-t
vertices by picking one arbitrary vertex,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j_{1}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j_{2}}
, for each edge-string of length
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{2}}}
in our vocabulary, we get a cover
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}\}\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}_{j_{1}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}j_{2}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}}}}\}
of size at most
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}
for the original graph.
Thus, it follows that
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{VC}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{V}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{T}}
.
∎

I.1
Proofs that String-lengths in
3
are Unique

We now show the technical sublemmas used in the previous proof.

SubLemma 2
.

For any
r\in\mathbb{N}
, there do not exist non-zero
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
such that:

\displaystyle i+j=r,\hskip 22.99988pti^{2}+j^{2}=r^{2}

(97)

Proof.

From
(i+j)^{2}=i^{2}+2ij+j^{2}
and the equations
i+j=r
and
i^{2}+j^{2}=r^{2}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle r^{2}=i^{2}+j^{2}+2ij=r^{2}+2ij\;\Rightarrow\;ij=0,

(98)

contradicting
i,j>0
.
∎

SubLemma 3
.

There do not exist two distinct pairs
\{i,j\}\neq\{a,b\}
of positive integers such that:

\displaystyle i+j=a+b,\hskip 22.99988pti^{2}+j^{2}=a^{2}+b^{2}

(99)

Proof.

Due to
i+j=a+b
, there is an integer
s
such that:

\displaystyle a=i-s,\hskip 22.99988ptb=j+s

(100)

Then, replacing
a
and
b
with their corresponding expressions from
eq.

100
, we obtain:

\displaystyle a^{2}+b^{2}-(i^{2}+j^{2})=(i-s)^{2}+(j+s)^{2}-i^{2}-j^{2}=2s^{2}+2s(j-i).

(101)

By the lemma statement

a^{2}+b^{2}=i^{2}+j^{2}
, so
2s^{2}+2s(j-i)=0
, i.e.:

\displaystyle s\bigl(s+j-i\bigr)=0.

(102)

Hence either
s=0
or
s=i-j
.
If
s=0
, then
a=i
and
b=j
.
If
s=i-j
, then
a=i-(i-j)=j
and
b=j+(i-j)=i
.
In both cases, it follows that
\{a,b\}=\{i,j\}
, contradicting distinctness.
∎

SubLemma 4
.

For any
r\in\mathbb{N}
, there do not exist non-zero
i,j,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that:

\displaystyle i+j+k=r,\hskip 22.99988pti^{2}+j^{2}+k^{2}=r^{2}

(103)

Proof.

Using
(i+j+k)^{2}=i^{2}+j^{2}+k^{2}+2(ij+ik+jk)
and the given equations:

\displaystyle r^{2}=r^{2}+2(ij+ik+jk)\implies ij+ik+jk=0.

(104)

With
i,j,k>0
, each product
ij,ik,jk
is positive, which yields a contradiction.
Hence, no solution exists.
∎

SubLemma 5
.

Let
r,p\in\mathbb{N}
. There do not exist non-zero
i,j,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that:

\displaystyle i+j+k=r+p,\hskip 22.99988pti^{2}+j^{2}+k^{2}=r^{2}+p^{2},\hskip 22.99988pti^{3}+j^{3}+k^{3}=r^{3}+p^{3}.

(105)

Proof.

Let
p_{1}=i+j+k
,
p_{2}=i^{2}+j^{2}+k^{2}
,
p_{3}=i^{3}+j^{3}+k^{3}
, and

e_{1}=i+j+k
,
e_{2}=ij+ik+jk
,
e_{3}=ijk
.
From the first two equations:

\displaystyle e_{1}=r+p,\hskip 22.99988pte_{2}=\frac{p_{1}^{2}-p_{2}}{2}=\frac{(r+p)^{2}-(r^{2}+p^{2})}{2}=rp.

(106)

Newton’s identity for three variables gives:

\displaystyle p_{3}=e_{1}p_{2}-e_{2}p_{1}+3e_{3}.

(107)

Substituting
p_{1}=r+p
,
p_{2}=r^{2}+p^{2}
,
e_{2}=rp
yields:

\displaystyle p_{3}=(r+p)(r^{2}+p^{2})-rp(r+p)+3e_{3}=(r+p)\bigl(r^{2}+p^{2}-rp\bigr)+3e_{3}=r^{3}+p^{3}+3e_{3}.

(108)

By the third equation in the lemma statement,
p_{3}=r^{3}+p^{3}
; hence
3e_{3}=0
and so
e_{3}=ijk=0
,
contradicting
i,j,k>0
.
∎

Appendix J
Definition of the Addition Chain Problem

An addition chain is a sequence of integers that provides an efficient way to “build” a target set of numbers starting from 1.

Definition 4
.

Let
\mathbf{t}=\{t_{1},t_{2},\dots,t_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}\}
be a finite set of positive integer targets.
An
addition chain
for
\mathbf{t}
is a sequence of integers
\mathbf{b}=\langle b_{0},b_{1},\dots,b_{R}\rangle
with the following properties:

1.

The sequence starts with
b_{0}=1
.

2.

Every subsequent element
b_{i}
is the sum of two preceding elements:

\displaystyle b_{i}=b_{j}+b_{k},\hskip 11.49994pt\text{for some }0\leq k\leq j<i.

(109)

3.

The sequence contains all targets: for every
t_{j}\in\mathbf{t}
, there is some
b_{r}\in\mathbf{b}
such that
t_{j}=b_{r}
.

4.

By convention, the
length
of the chain
\mathbf{b}
is
R
.

Definition 5
.

Let
\mathbf{t}
be a set of positive integers.
Given a maximum length
\zeta
, the
addition chain decision problem (
add-chain
)
requires deciding whether there exists
an addition chain
for
\mathbf{t}
with length at most
\zeta
.
The
addition chain optimisation problem
is to find the minimal length for such an addition chain.

We denote by
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{AddChain}}(\mathbf{t},\zeta)
a function which returns
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{T}}
if such a chain exists (meaning the
add-chain
instance is satisfied), and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{F}}
otherwise.

Appendix K
Proof that the Unary OPE Decision Problem is (at Least) Weakly NP-Complete

See
6

Proof.

We write
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\mathtt{OPE}}^{1}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})

for a function which returns
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{T}}
if its input corresponds to a satisfiable instance of the
OPE-1-TOK
decision problem, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{F}}
otherwise. To prove weak
\mathsf{NP}
-completeness, we must show that the problem is in
\mathsf{NP}
and that it is weakly
\mathsf{NP}
-hard. Inclusion in
\mathsf{NP}
was already established by
10
. We prove weak
\mathsf{NP}
-hardness via a reduction from the
add-chain
decision problem. First, we define this reduction.

Reduction 4
.

Given an instance of
add-chain
consisting of a targets
\mathbf{t}=\{t_{1},\dots,t_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}\}
and a length limit
\zeta
, we construct an instance of the
OPE-1-TOK
problem with the following parameters. The dataset
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}
is the set of unary strings corresponding to the targets:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}a}^{t_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}a}^{t_{2}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}a}^{t_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}}}\}
;
the merge budget is set to the addition chain length limit:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}=\zeta
;
and the token count threshold is set to the number of targets
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}
.

Note that setting the threshold
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
to the number of strings in the dataset implies that a valid solution must represent every string as a single token. The proof proceeds in two parts, showing both directions of the equivalence:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{AddChain}}(\mathbf{t},\zeta)\iff{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\mathtt{OPE}}^{1}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})

(110)

Forward Step (
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{AddChain}}(\mathbf{t},\zeta)\implies{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\mathtt{OPE}}^{1}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})
).

We first show that a solution to the
add-chain
problem implies a solution to the
OPE-1-TOK
problem.
Assume there exists a valid addition chain
\mathbf{b}^{\star}=\langle b_{0},b_{1},\dots,b_{R}\rangle

of length
R\leq\zeta
for the target set
\mathbf{t}
.
By definition, for each element
b^{\star}_{r}\in\mathbf{b}^{\star}
(where
r\geq 1
), there exist indices
r^{\prime},r^{\prime\prime}<r
such that
b^{\star}_{r}=b^{\star}_{r^{\prime}}+b^{\star}_{r^{\prime\prime}}
.
We construct a merge sequence
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}=\langle{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}m}_{1},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}m}_{R}\rangle
of length
R
where each merge is defined as
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}m}_{r}=({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{b^{\star}_{r^{\prime}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{b^{\star}_{r^{\prime\prime}}}})
, corresponding to the predecessors of
b_{r}
in the addition chain.
The length of this merge sequence
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}
is
R\leq\zeta
, satisfying the merge budget
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}=\zeta
.
By the iterative definition of the merge-extracted vocabulary, the resulting vocabulary
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{b^{\star}_{r^{\prime}}+b^{\star}_{r^{\prime\prime}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}m}_{r}\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}\}
will contain a token
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{b^{\star}_{r}}
for every element
b^{\star}_{r}
in the addition chain
\mathbf{b}^{\star}
.
Since the addition chain
\mathbf{b}^{\star}
contains all targets
t_{j}\in\mathbf{t}
, the vocabulary
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}
is guaranteed to contain a single token for each target string
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{t_{j}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}
.
Consequently, the direct encoding function
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}]
can represent each string
{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}
with exactly one token. The total token count is therefore:

\displaystyle\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}}|{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}]({\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}})|=\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}}1=|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}|

(111)

By the construction used in our reduction,
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}|={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
. The condition is met, thus proving the implication.

Backward Step (
{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{Tok}_{\mathtt{OPE}}^{1}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta})\implies{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathrm{AddChain}}(\mathbf{t},\zeta)
).

Next, we show that a solution to the
OPE-1-TOK
problem implies a solution to the
add-chain
problem.
Assume there exists a merge sequence
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}_{\mathtt{opt}}}
of length
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}=R\leq\zeta
that satisfies the
OPE-1-TOK
decision problem.
The condition is:

\displaystyle\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}}|{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}]({\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}})|\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}

(112)

where, by the reduction’s construction, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}=|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}|
.
Since the tokenisation of any string must contain at least one token, this sum is also lower-bounded by
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}|
.
Therefore, the inequality must hold with equality, which is only possible if every string is tokenised into exactly one token:

\displaystyle|{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}{\color[rgb]{0.9961,0.3789,0}\mathtt{tok}}_{\text{\pointer}}}[{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}]({\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}})|=1\hskip 11.49994pt\text{for all }{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}c}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{D}}.

(113)

This implies that for every target
t_{j}\in\mathbf{t}
, the corresponding string

{\color[rgb]{0.3281,0.4961,0.9375}a}^{t_{j}}
must exist as a single token in the merge-extracted vocabulary, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{t_{j}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}
.
Now, construct an addition chain from
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}
as:
\mathbf{b}=[\ell_{r}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}_{r}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}]
.
The construction of
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}
from merges guarantees that
\mathbf{b}
is a valid addition chain.
12
12

12

If any subword produced by a non-reachable merge exists in
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}
, it should be pruned from
\mathbf{b}
. A non-reachable merge is a merge
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}m}_{r}=({\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}}_{r^{\prime}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\circledcirc}}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}}_{r^{\prime\prime}}}})
whose pair of subwords
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}}_{r^{\prime}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\ell}}_{r^{\prime\prime}}}
cannot be generated.

Since
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}\mathbf{m}}}
contains all strings
{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}a}^{\color[rgb]{0.8633,0.1484,0.4961}{t_{j}}}
, then
\mathbf{b}
must contain all targets
t_{j}\in\mathbf{t}
.
Further,
\mathbf{b}
was constructed from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}K}\leq\zeta
merges.
There is thus an addition chain for
\mathbf{t}
of length at most
\zeta
, which concludes the proof.
∎
